ATS is careers.bcfsa.ca (SuccessFactors-style); BCFSA states it works with SEVERAL EXTERNAL RECRUITING PARTNERS, so an agency screener may parse the resume before BCFSA does - parse-safety was weighted accordingly and the first call should be assumed non-technical. POSTED $56,294-$85,362 (midpoint $70,828); xlsx records "56K to 85K". CLOSE DATE AUG 25 2026 - hard deadline, not a rolling posting, so submit and send outreach in the same 48 hours. STEP 0 = APPLY, near the top of the fit distribution and arguably the best-matched junior posting in the repo: the JD states TWICE that academic projects and portfolio work count as valid evidence of readiness, which is exactly this profile's shape, and every hard requirement clears honestly (B.Tech IT + MPS Data Analytics; SQL named before Python in the JD and both proven in bullets; relational data / data quality / transformation / modelling / APIs / reporting datasets each have a supporting bullet; Vancouver BC resident against a BC-residency proviso). Not enrollment-gated, so the graduated-Jun-2026 constraint does not apply. USER DECISIONS 2026-08-11: "complete all the steps" (full pipeline, no per-step pauses); the two Step 0 questions went unanswered so it ran on stated assumptions - NO known BCFSA contact (cold application) and work authorization clear (nothing about status appears in any document; still worth confirming before submitting). THE RESEARCH FINDING THAT DRIVES THE WHOLE APPLICATION (sourced, bcfsa.ca regulatory statement + advisory): the 2026 REAL ESTATE BROKERAGE DATA CALL is the JD's abstract phrase "regulated submissions" made concrete - every B.C. trading-services brokerage and managing broker must file residential sales and Home Buyer Rescission Period data through IRIS (Integrated Regulatory Information System) by Apr 17 2026, brokerages with nothing to report must file a NIL submission, Greater Vancouver Realtors members are exempt for in-region sales but not outside it, and failure to respond can draw administrative penalties. That is a provenance-and-exemption-logic problem before it is a reporting problem, and it opens the cover letter. SECOND SOURCED ANGLE: BCFSA and Greater Vancouver Realtors signed an information-sharing partnership to enhance data-driven oversight and REDUCE REPORTING BURDEN - they are ingesting an external partner's feed to stop brokerages reporting the same thing twice, which is what the GVR exemption above actually is. That maps to the JD's ingestion-and-integration accountability and carries cover-letter para 3. Also: first-ever published three-year Strategic Plan "What Matters. Made Clear." (2026-29); real estate regulator integration now complete (which is why the JD has a whole modernization/migration accountability); BC's Top Employers 2025 + defined-benefit pension (deliberately NOT used as a stated motivation - citing benefits reads as self-interested at a consumer-protection regulator). BCFSA is ALSO hiring "Senior and Data Engineer Roles (Permanent and Contract)" in the same department, so the data team is being built out, not backfilled. THE GAP THAT DECIDES THIS APPLICATION: AZURE. The JD's migration list is SQL Server, Synapse, Dataverse, SharePoint, Databricks, Fabric, plus Azure Data Factory / Azure Data Lake / Azure DevOps - unambiguously a Microsoft shop - and every cloud noun in the profile is AWS, GCP or Supabase. Nothing was faked; no Azure keyword appears anywhere on the resume. Crucially the JD PRE-FORGIVES this ("exposure ... is an asset; direct professional experience with every tool is not expected"), so it is conceded out loud in cover-letter para 4 alongside the honest transferable argument: same pattern (files landing in object storage triggering orchestrated Spark jobs), Spark itself via AWS Glue, MySQL stored procedures mapping onto T-SQL, and GitHub feature-branch/PR review discipline standing in for Azure DevOps Boards. Say "Spark" out loud early in any screen, BEFORE Databricks comes up - that single reframe converts "wrong stack" into "same engine, different workspace" and is worth more than any line on the page. SECOND-LARGEST WEAKNESS, and the most addressable: DOCUMENTATION ARTIFACTS. This JD mentions documentation ~8 times (data dictionaries, runbooks, technical notes, issue logs, decision records, lineage notes, glossary terms) because at a regulator writing it down IS the deliverable, and the database has no data dictionary or runbook recorded anywhere. "Data dictionaries" was briefly drafted into the Skills row and then REMOVED for having no backing. The closest real artifact is the Seaweed per-chart citation footnotes separating seaweed-specific figures from all-aquaculture proxies - a written caveat created specifically so a claim could be defended - which was cut from the resume for space and therefore carries cover-letter para 3 instead. ACTION: if any project README documents a schema, that counts as a data dictionary and should go into database/master-project.md. STRONGEST ASSET, and it is rare on a junior resume: LINEAGE. The AWS ETL bullet (100% key uniqueness across 5.6M records via deterministic MD5 IDs for keyless rows, dedup and null filtering in Spark, ingestion timestamp AND source-file lineage stamped on every item for auditability) is the single best line on the page for a regulator, and lineage is surfaced three times (Summary, Skills, bullet). Paired with the ~$120K CAD misapplied-tax audit (root cause traced to tax-exempt groceries billed at Vancouver's 12% rate, then both the pricing logic and the historical records fixed), the resume answers "fair, timely, explainable and defensible decisions" with mechanisms instead of sentiment. DELIBERATE COVERAGE TRADE (keyword-coverage.md, per the skill's rule that the human read wins): the resume never uses "support", "assist" or "help" even though the JD's accountabilities are written almost entirely in those verbs - mirroring them would satisfy a naive keyword match and damage the Step 6 read, since ownership verbs are what make a junior look ready. Also deliberately NOT led with: the LLM/RAG/agent work, which is the most impressive thing in the profile and the least relevant here (this JD names ML and generative AI exactly once, as a growth interest, never as a duty) - leading with it invites "will leave for an AI job in six months" on a 12-month contract. OTHER GAPS, none claimed: SQL Server/T-SQL (MySQL stored-procedure work is the honest adjacency), Databricks (Spark via Glue is), Fabric (Power BI is), Dataverse/SharePoint (nothing), formal data catalogue/glossary/classification tooling (hand-rolled lineage is), runbooks, "regression tests" as a phrase (55+ pytest/Vitest unit tests protecting transforms across releases is the real thing), data contracts in the governance sense (Pydantic typed API contracts + a stable downstream SQL view are contract-shaped but not that), public-sector employment (partial match, and a good one: 7 Statistics Canada / DFO / Nova Scotia government licensing datasets integrated with defensibility footnotes), and JUPYTER NOTEBOOKS - named in the JD's first accountability, almost certainly true, and absent from every master file, so it stayed off the resume. Remote role, Montreal HQ, so the Surrey BC base is a non-issue. THE STRATEGY, and the one line that makes this application non-absurd: the JD's Responsibilities contain a quietly-stated hidden requirement, 'Understand domain data sets and their characteristics such that data sets with similar characteristics may be generated'. That is a DISTRIBUTION-CHARACTERIZATION ask, not a pixels ask, and the Credit Risk pipeline answers it near-verbatim - profiling nulls and NaNs across all 151 source columns in one Spark agg and classifying sparsity BY CAUSE (no-information, structural, informative-missingness, vintage-driven) rather than by a blanket threshold. Same intellectual move, wrong data type. It is the lead bullet of the resume and the spine of the cover letter. Nothing in any artifact claims vision, pharma, GxP, Keras, TensorFlow or Caffe. RESEARCH WIN #1 AND THE MOST ACTIONABLE ITEM IN THIS ROW - FRENCH: GenAIz's SIBLING requisition 'AI DNN Applied Researcher' (Canada remote, 5+ yrs, Master's or PhD, image-processing DNNs, Transformers named, IDENTICAL stale framework list) explicitly states FRENCH REQUIRED. This posting does not mention French at all. Candidate does not speak French. TREAT AS AN UNRESOLVED KNOCKOUT - it is the cheapest disqualifying question available and is scripted as the FIRST thing to ask on any call (phone-screen-narrative.md section 0) and as one of two questions in the InMail. If French is required this application is dead regardless of the vision gap and no further effort is warranted. RESEARCH WIN #2 - GHOST-JOB EVIDENCE IS CONCRETE, not a hunch: the sibling AI req carries a 76% ghost-job score on Remote Rocketship, has been OPEN 188 DAYS, and the aggregator reports ELEVEN VACANCIES AGAINST A 17-PERSON TEAM, with no salary transparency. Both AI postings also list CAFFE, dead since ~2018 (absorbed into PyTorch via Caffe2), so the JDs are recycled boilerplate. Expect a low response rate that has nothing to do with candidate quality. Send outreach ONCE, then move on; a second nudge is wasted effort. COMPANY: GenAIz, Montreal, founded 2016, a division of consultancy Uni3T; AI automation for life sciences/pharma with five products (ConformityCheck.AI, RiskRelief.AI, FileSub.AI, DueDil.AI, LitReview.AI), GxP-compliant positioning, 100,000 words/second, ingests Word/Excel/SharePoint/Google Drive/S3. No funding announcements and no dated press found for a 2016 company - likely bootstrapped/consultancy-funded. Flagship public case study is CELLCARTA (automating the T Cell Health Index so Phase 2 evidence might let trials bypass Phase 3; replaced manual instrument-specific data cleaning and cross-source quality review) - that is the cover letter's only company-specific claim and it is sourced. THE IMAGE QUESTION IS UNRESOLVED AND IS LABELLED AN INFERENCE IN company-research.md, NEVER ASSERTED AS FACT: their entire public product surface is text/document-centric with NO mention of images, vision, OCR or synthetic data, yet two reqs want image DNNs. Two candidate readings - (a) DOCUMENT IMAGERY, a third-party GxP source describes GenAIz tooling digitizing and reviewing PAPER BATCH RECORDS, which are confidential handwritten-plus-printed forms, exactly the case where you must GENERATE synthetic training data; or (b) ASSAY/PATHOLOGY IMAGERY, since CellCarta runs digital pathology and spatial biology (imageDx PRISM, Lunit partnership). Asking WHICH is one of the two InMail questions - it signals reading past the JD and costs nothing if wrong.
